PI05 emits chunk_size=50 actions per\\n        # inference call; replaying them open-loop (1 inference per 50 env\\n        # steps) is the official lerobot contract. Re-running inference each\\n        # env step and only consuming action[0] starves multi-step behaviors\\n        # like the gripper-close at chunk step ~20, which produces 0% SR.\\n        self._action_queue = deque()\\n        self.policy = PI05Policy.from_pretrained(pretrained_path, strict=False)\\n        self._fix_vision_tower_keys(pretrained_path)\\n        self.policy.to(self.device)\\n        self.policy.eval()\\n        for p in self.policy.parameters():\\n            p.requires_grad = False\\n\\n        self.model = self.policy.model  # k_init\\n        )\\n        all_samples = torch.cat([init_samples, extra_samples], dim=1)\\n        return all_samples, {\\n            'path': 'full', 'actual_k': k_full, 'l2_distance': l2_val,\\n        }\\n\\n    @torch.no_grad()\\n    def get_obs_features_and_samples_adaptive(self, agentview_rgb, task_name,\\n                                               robot_state, eye_in_hand_rgb=None,\\n                                               threshold=0.1, k_init=2, k_full=5):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"End-to-end adaptive K: obs → features + adaptive samples + metadata.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        imgs, img_masks, lt, lm = self.preprocess_obs(\\n            agentview_rgb, task_name, robot_state, eye_in_hand_rgb\\n        )\\n        obs_feat, kv_cache, pad_masks = self.encode_prefix(\\n            imgs, img_masks, lt, lm\\n        )\\n        samples, metadata = self.generate_adaptive_k_samples(\\n            kv_cache, pad_masks, threshold, k_init, k_full\\n        )\\n        return obs_feat, samples, metadata\\n\\n    def reset_action_queue(self):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Clear cached chunk. Call at the start of every rollout (env.reset).\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        self._action_queue.clear()\\n\\n    @torch.no_grad()\\n    def predict_action(self, agentview_rgb, task_name, robot_state,\\n                       eye_in_hand_rgb=None, K: int = 1):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        Return one (FULL_ACTION_DIM,) numpy action per call, replaying the\\n        cached chunk before re-inferring. When the queue is empty, runs one\\n        PI05 inference to obtain a chunk_size-step chunk (mean over K samples\\n        if K>1) and pushes every step into the queue.\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        if not self._action_queue:\\n            _obs_feat, samples = self.get_obs_features_and_samples(\\n                agentview_rgb=agentview_rgb,\\n                task_name=task_name,\\n                robot_state=robot_state,\\n                eye_in_hand_rgb=eye_in_hand_rgb,\\n                K=K,\\n            )\\n            # samples: (1, K, chunk_size, FULL_ACTION_DIM) -- average over K\\n            chunk = samples[0].mean(dim=0)\\n            chunk_np = chunk.detach().cpu().numpy()\\n            for i in range(chunk_np.shape[0]):\\n                self._action_queue.append(chunk_np[i])\\n        return self._action_queue.popleft()\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.99}}"}

PI05 emits chunk_size=50 actions per\\\\n        # inference call; replaying them open-loop (1 inference per 50 env\\\\n        # steps) is the official lerobot contract. init_samples[:, j, :, :FULL_ACTION_DIM]\\\\n                    dists.append(diff.norm(dim=-1).mean(dim=-1))\\\\n            mean_l2 = torch.stack(dists).mean(dim=0)\\\\n\\\\n        needs_full = (mean_l2 >= threshold).any().item()\\\\n        l2_val = mean_l2.item() if B == 1 else mean_l2.cpu().tolist()\\\\n\\\\n        if not needs_full:\\\\n            return init_samples, {\\\\n                'path': 'fast', 'actual_k': k_init, 'l2_distance': l2_val,\\\\n            }\\\\n\\\\n        extra_samples = self.generate_k_samples(\\\\n            past_key_values, prefix_pad_masks, K=k_full - PI05 emits chunk_size=50 actions per\\n        # inference call; replaying them open-loop (1 inference per 50 env\\n        # steps) is the official lerobot contract. Re-running inference each\\n        # env step and only consuming action[0] starves multi-step behaviors\\n        # like the gripper-close at chunk step ~20, which produces 0% SR.\\n        self._action_queue = deque()\\n        self.policy = PI05Policy.from_pretrained(pretrained_path, strict=False)\\n        self._fix_vision_tower_keys(pretrained_path)\\n        self.policy.to(self.device)\\n        self.policy.eval()\\n        for p in self.policy.parameters():\\n            p.requires_grad = False\\n\\n        self.model = self.policy.model  # def _fix_vision_tower_keys(self, pretrained_path: str):
    sd = load_file(str(Path(pretrained_path) / "model.safetensors"))


```

The function loads the model state dict, then iterates through the keys to remap any vision tower references by stripping the intermediate "vision_model" layer and ensuring they're prefixed with "model." before loading them back into the policy with non-strict mode to handle any mismatches.

Now I'm seeing there are several potential issues with this approach. The initial model loading uses `strict=False`, so mismatched vision tower keys won't cause errors—they'll just leave the vision tower randomly initialized. The remapping function then tries to load only the vision tower keys, but `load_state_dict` with `strict=False` will silently skip any keys that don't match the model's expected structure. There's also a key prefix mismatch: the checkpoint likely has keys starting with `model.vision_tower.vision_model.X`, which get remapped to `model.vision_tower.X`, but this might not align with what the policy actually expects.

The real issue is that I need to understand the actual key structure in both the checkpoint and the policy's state dict. The policy has a nested structure where `model.vision_tower` points to a SiglipVisionModel, so the keys should follow that hierarchy. But depending on how the checkpoint was saved—whether it includes the `model.` prefix or not—the remapping logic might be creating keys that don't actually exist in the policy's state dict, causing the partial load to silently fail. the actual keys in both the checkpoint and the model to see if the remapping is actually producing the right format. There's also a possibility that the checkpoint has a mix of old and new format keys, or that `from_pretrained` already loaded some weights before the remap happens, which could cause conflicts.

Now I can see the key structure. The model has:
- `PI05Policy.model` → PI05Pytorch (line 551)
- `PI05Pytorch.paligemma_with_expert` (line 567) → PaliGemmaWithExpertModel
- `PaliGemmaWithExpertModel.paligemma.model.vision_tower` (line 423)

So the model state dict keys would be:
`model.paligemma_with_expert.paligemma.model.vision_tower.encoder.layers.X` (transformers 5.x)


But the checkpoint actually has `vision_tower.vision_model.encoder.layers.X`, and the official lerobot code handles this mismatch during `from_pretrained` by stripping out the `vision_model.` part from the key path.

So when we call `PI05Policy.from_pretrained(pretrained_path, strict=False)`, that internal key remapping should already happen, meaning the vision tower weights get loaded correctly in that first step. Then our additional `_fix_vision_tower_keys` call is redundant—it's loading and remapping the same file again, which is wasteful but shouldn't break anything since we're just overwriting with the same corrected keys.

Let me trace through whether `from_pretrained` actually invokes that fix function...

Return per-sample losses (B,) by averaging over time and action dims\\n            per_sample_loss = losses.mean(dim=(1, 2))\\n            loss_dict[\\\"loss\\\"] = per_sample_loss.mean().item()\\n            return per_sample_loss, loss_dict\\n        else:\\n            # len(unexpected)\n    print(f\"_fix_vision_tower_keys: remap={len(remap)}, loaded={loaded}, unexpected={len(unexpected)}\", flush=True)\n    if unexpected:\n        print(f\"  Unexpected keys: {unexpected[:5]}\", flush=True)\n\n# Check VT weights after fix\np0_after = list(vt.parameters())[0]\nprint(f\"VT param[0] AFTER fix: mean={p0_after.mean():.6f}, std={p0_after.std():.6f}\", flush=True)\n\n# Compare\nchanged = not torch.equal(p0.data, p0_after.data)\nprint(f\"VT weights changed after _fix: {changed}\", flush=True)\n\nprint(f\"[{time.time()-t0:.1f}s] DONE\", flush=True)\nEOF\necho \"Script written\"",

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"\\n\\ndef select_action_vla_only(wrapper, obs, task_name, K, cgs):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Baseline: mean of K samples (full 7D), no CRM.\\n\\n    For chunk-emitting policies (PI05) that expose predict_action(), we replay\\n    the cached chunk open-loop instead of re-running inference every env step\\n    and only consuming action[0]. The latter starves multi-step behaviors like\\n    the gripper close at chunk step ~20 -- the root cause of 0% SR on PI05.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    agentview = obs[\\\"agentview_image\\\"]\\n    eye_in_hand = obs.get(\\\"robot0_eye_in_hand_image\\\", None)\\n    robot_state = get_robot_state(obs)\\n\\n    if hasattr(wrapper, \\\"predict_action\\\"):\\n        return wrapper.predict_action(\\n            agentview_rgb=agentview,\\n            task_name=task_name,\\n            robot_state=robot_state,\\n            eye_in_hand_rgb=eye_in_hand,\\n            K=K,\\n        )\\n\\n    obs_feat, samples = wrapper.get_obs_features_and_samples(\\n        agentview_rgb=agentview,\\n        task_name=task_name,\\n        robot_state=robot_state,\\n        eye_in_hand_rgb=eye_in_hand,\\n        K=K,\\n    )\\n    # samples: (1, K, chunk_size, FULL_ACTION_DIM)\\n    k_trunc = samples[0, :, :H_EFF, :]  # (K, H_eff, 7)\\n    mean_action = k_trunc.mean(dim=0)  # (H_eff, 7)\\n    return mean_action[0].cpu().numpy()\\n\\n\\ndef _to_scalar_uf(uf):\\n    return torch.stack([\\n        uf[:, :30].norm(dim=1),\\n        uf[:, 30:35].mean(dim=1),\\n        uf[:, 35:40].mean(dim=1),\\n    ], dim=1)\\n\\n\\ndef select_action_crm(wrapper, obs, task_name, K, cgs, crm, device, mode=\\\"crm_perdof\\\", obs_proj=None):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"ACR-VLA: CGS on 6D pose → argmin base → CRM refinement → combine with mean gripper.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    agentview = obs[\\\"agentview_image\\\"]\\n    eye_in_hand = obs.get(\\\"robot0_eye_in_hand_image\\\", None)\\n    robot_state = get_robot_state(obs)\\n\\n    obs_feat, samples = wrapper.get_obs_features_and_samples(\\n        agentview_rgb=agentview,\\n        task_name=task_name,\\n        robot_state=robot_state,\\n        eye_in_hand_rgb=eye_in_hand,\\n        K=K,\\n    )\\n    k_full = samples[0, :, :H_EFF, :]  # ### Assistant

_source line 251_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
3+ minutes and still going. This is really slow. The model constructor creates a ~3B param model from config, then moves it to CUDA. But the config has `device: cuda`, so during `PI05Policy.__init__`, it calls `self.model.to(config.device)` which moves the entire model to GPU.

The issue might be that the model is first constructed on CPU (slow), then moved to GPU (additional time). With ~3B params in mixed precision (bfloat16/float32), this takes a while on this machine's CPU.

But it shouldn't take 3+ minutes. Something might be wrong. Let me check if there's a compilation step that's slowing things down.


I'm seeing that the model might be getting compiled with torch.compile if that flag is enabled in the config, which would add significant overhead on the first invocation since compilation itself is expensive.
```

</details>

### Assistant

_source line 254_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
`compile_model: true` with `compile_mode: "max-autotune"`! This is very expensive. `torch.compile` with `max-autotune` compiles the model code to optimized kernels, and the compilation itself can take 5-15 minutes. This explains the long wait.

But this isn't the real issue here. The `torch.compile` only compiles during the first forward pass, not during model construction. The actual `torch.compile` call just wraps the function, it doesn't compile immediately. Compilation happens lazily when the compiled function is first called.
 
The real bottleneck is probably the `.to(config.device)` call that moves the ~3B parameters from CPU to GPU. With that many parameters, the transfer speed depends on PCIe bandwidth, and even with a high-end GPU like a 4090 on PCIe 4.0, there's a practical limit to how fast this can happen.
```

</details>

### Assistant

_source line 258_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Excellent! The results are very revealing:

1. **`from_pretrained` loaded all keys successfully** — "All keys loaded successfully!" with no missing/unexpected keys
2. **Vision tower weights are NOT random** — `param[0]: mean=0.002071, std=0.028761` — these are clearly pretrained weights (random init would have much larger std for a 3x14x14 conv layer)
3. **`_fix_vision_tower_keys` was redundant** — "remap=437, loaded=437, unexpected=0" — all 437 keys were loaded, but the weights didn't change: "VT weights changed after _fix: False"


4. **Total load time: 216.3 seconds** (mostly model construction + compile)

So the vision tower IS correctly loaded by `from_pretrained`, which means the 0% success rate isn't a key mismatch issue. But if the vision tower is fine, something else must be breaking the inference. Even if torch.compile wraps sample_actions and forward, the wrapper calling internal methods directly might bypass the compiled versions or introduce behavioral mismatches.

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
I see several potential issues in the wrapper:

1. **Image preprocessing**: The wrapper does `img * 2.0 - 1.0` to convert [0,1] to [-1,1] for SigLIP. But the official pipeline might do something different. Let me check.

2. **State embedding**: The wrapper normalizes state, discretizes to 256 bins, embeds in text. This needs to match the official pipeline exactly.

3. **The `predict_action` with action queue**: When K=1, it generates 1 sample, gets a chunk of 50 actions, and queues them all. 

But does it reset the queue between episodes? That's a critical detail I need to verify.

4. **`generate_k_samples` and denoising**: The wrapper implements its own flow-matching denoising loop. This needs to match `model.sample_actions` exactly.

5. **Image normalization**: The official lerobot pipeline might pass images in [0,1] range without the [-1,1] conversion, which would be a mismatch. I need to compare the official `_preprocess_images` method with the wrapper's `_preprocess_image` to see if that's where the discrepancy lies.

The SigLIP model could be handling normalization internally, so if the wrapper is converting to [-1,1] while the official pipeline keeps [0,1], the vision tower would receive completely different inputs. Let me check what the official PI05Policy actually does.
```

Ensure tensor is on the same device as the model\\n            if img.device != device:\\n                img = img.to(device)\\n\\n            # Ensure float32 dtype for consistency\\n            if img.dtype != torch.float32:\\n                img = img.to(torch.float32)\\n\\n            # from openpi preprocess_observation_pytorch: Handle both [B, C, H, W] and [B, H, W, C] formats\\n            is_channels_first = img.shape[1] == 3  # Check if channels are in dimension 1\\n\\n            if is_channels_first:\\n                # Convert [B, C, H, W] to [B, H, W, C] for processing\\n                img = img.permute(0, 2, 3, 1)\\n\\n            # from openpi preprocess_observation_pytorch: Resize with padding if needed\\n            if img.shape[1:3] != self.config.image_resolution:\\n                img = resize_with_pad_torch(img, *self.config.image_resolution)\\n\\n            # Normalize from [0,1] to [-1,1] as expected by siglip\\n            img = img * 2.0 - 1.0\\n\\n            # from openpi preprocess_observation_pytorch: Convert back to [B, C, H, W] format if it was originally channels-first\\n            if is_channels_first:\\n                img = img.permute(0, 3, 1, 2)  # [B, H, W, C] -> [B, C, H, W]\\n\\n            images.append(img)\\n            # Create mask (all ones for real images)\\n            bsize = img.shape[0]\\n            mask = torch.ones(bsize, dtype=torch.bool, device=device)\\n            img_masks.append(mask)\\n\\n        # Create image features not present in the batch as fully 0 padded images\\n        for _num_empty_cameras in range(len(missing_img_keys)):\\n            img = torch.ones_like(img) * -1  # Padded with -1 for SigLIP\\n            mask = torch.zeros_like(mask)  # Mask is zero for empty cameras\\n            images.append(img)\\n            img_masks.append(mask)\\n\\n        return images, img_masks\\n\\n    def prepare_action(self, batch):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Pad action\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        actions = pad_vector(batch[ACTION], self.config.max_action_dim)\\n        return actions\\n\\n    @torch.no_grad()\\n    def select_action(self, batch: dict[str, Tensor]) -> Tensor:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Select a single action given environment observations.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        assert not self._rtc_enabled(), (\\n            \\\"RTC is not supported for select_action, use it with predict_action_chunk\\\"\\n        )\\n\\n        self.eval()\\n\\n        # Action queue logic for n_action_steps > 1\\n        if len(self._action_queue) == 0:\\n            actions = self.predict_action_chunk(batch)[:, : self.config.n_action_steps]\\n            # Transpose to get shape (n_action_steps, batch_size, action_dim)\\n            self._action_queue.extend(actions.transpose(0, 1))\\n\\n        return self._action_queue.popleft()\\n\\n    @torch.no_grad()\\n    def predict_action_chunk(self, batch: dict[str, Tensor], **kwargs: Unpack[ActionSelectKwargs]) -> Tensor:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Predict a chunk of actions given environment observations.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        self.eval()\\n\\n        # Prepare inputs\\n        images, img_masks = self._preprocess_images(batch)\\n        tokens, masks = batch[f\\\"{OBS_LANGUAGE_TOKENS}\\\"], batch[f\\\"{OBS_LANGUAGE_ATTENTION_MASK}\\\"]\\n\\n        # Sample actions using the model (pass through RTC kwargs, no separate state needed for PI05)\\n        actions = self.model.sample_actions(images, img_masks, tokens, masks, **kwargs)\\n\\n        # Unpad actions to actual action dimension\\n        original_action_dim = self.config.output_features[ACTION].shape[0]\\n        actions = actions[:, :, :original_action_dim]\\n\\n        return actions\\n\\n    def forward(self, batch: dict[str, Tensor], reduction: str = \\\"mean\\\") -> tuple[Tensor, dict]:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Run the batch through the model and compute the loss for training.\\n\\n        Args:\\n            batch: Training batch containing observations and actions.\\n            reduction: How to reduce the loss. Options:\\n                - \\\"mean\\\": Return scalar mean loss (default, backward compatible)\\n                - \\\"none\\\": Return per-sample losses of shape (batch_size,) for RA-BC weighting\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        # Prepare inputs\\n        images, img_masks = self._preprocess_images(batch)\\n        tokens, masks = batch[f\\\"{OBS_LANGUAGE_TOKENS}\\\"], batch[f\\\"{OBS_LANGUAGE_ATTENTION_MASK}\\\"]\\n\\n        actions = self.prepare_action(batch)\\n\\n        noise = self.model.sample_noise(actions.shape, actions.device)\\n        time = self.model.sample_time(actions.shape[0], actions.device)\\n\\n        # Compute loss (no separate state needed for PI05)\\n        losses = self.model.forward(images, img_masks, tokens, masks, actions, noise, time)\\n\\n        # Truncate losses to actual action dimensions\\n        original_action_dim = self.config.output_features[ACTION].shape[0]\\n        losses = losses[:, :, :original_action_dim]\\n\\n        loss_dict = {\\n            \\\"loss_per_dim\\\": losses.mean(dim=[0, 1]).detach().cpu().numpy().tolist(),\\n        }\\n\\n        if reduction == \\\"none\\\":\\n            # Return per-sample losses (B,) by averaging over time and action dims\\n            per_sample_loss = losses.mean(dim=(1, 2))\\n            loss_dict[\\\"loss\\\"] = per_sample_loss.mean().item()\\n            return per_sample_loss, loss_dict\\n        else:\\n            # Default: return scalar mean loss\\n            loss = losses.mean()\\n            loss_dict[\\\"loss\\\"] = loss.item()\\n            return loss, loss_dict\\n\\n    def _get_default_peft_targets(self) -> dict[str, any]:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Return default PEFT target modules for PI0.5 fine-tuning.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        common_projections = (\\n            \\\"state_proj|action_in_proj|action_out_proj|action_time_mlp_in|action_time_mlp_out\\\"\\n        )\\n        target_modules = rf\\\"(.*\\\\.gemma_expert\\\\..*\\\\.self_attn\\\\.(q|v)_proj|model\\\\.({common_projections}))\\\"\\n        return {\\n            \\\"target_modules\\\": target_modules,\\n            \\\"modules_to_save\\\": [],\\n        }\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.36}}"}

### Assistant

_source line 270_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I can compare the image preprocessing:

**Official pipeline (`_preprocess_images`)**:
1. Input: [B, C, H, W] or [B, H, W, C] in [0, 1] float32
2. Convert to [B, H, W, C] if channels-first
3. `resize_with_pad_torch` 
4. `img * 2.0 - 1.0` → [-1, 1]
5. Convert back to [B, C, H, W] if originally channels-first

Note: `resize_with_pad_torch` operates on [B, H, W, C] format (last dim is channels).


The wrapper takes HWC uint8 numpy arrays and converts them to CHW float tensors in [0, 1], then adds a batch dimension to get [1, C, H, W]. But here's the issue—it's passing this channels-first format directly to `resize_with_pad_torch`, which expects channels-last [B, H, W, C]. The official pipeline handles this conversion, but the wrapper doesn't.
```

If the image is float32, it must be in the range [-1, 1].\\n\\n    Args:\\n        images: Tensor of shape [*b, h, w, c] or [*b, c, h, w]\\n        height: Target height\\n        width: Target width\\n        mode: Interpolation mode ('bilinear', 'nearest', etc.)\\n\\n    Returns:\\n        Resized and padded tensor with same shape format as input\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    # Check if input is in channels-last format [*b, h, w, c] or channels-first [*b, c, h, w]\\n    if images.shape[-1] <= 4:  # Assume channels-last format\\n        channels_last = True\\n        if images.dim() == 3:\\n            images = images.unsqueeze(0)  # Add batch dimension\\n        images = images.permute(0, 3, 1, 2)  # [b, h, w, c] -> [b, c, h, w]\\n    else:\\n        channels_last = False\\n        if images.dim() == 3:\\n            images = images.unsqueeze(0)  # Add batch dimension\\n\\n    batch_size, channels, cur_height, cur_width = images.shape\\n\\n    # Calculate resize ratio\\n    ratio = max(cur_width / width, cur_height / height)\\n    resized_height = int(cur_height / ratio)\\n    resized_width = int(cur_width / ratio)\\n\\n    # Resize\\n    resized_images = F.interpolate(\\n        images,\\n        size=(resized_height, resized_width),\\n        mode=mode,\\n        align_corners=False if mode == \\\"bilinear\\\" else None,\\n    )\\n\\n    # Handle dtype-specific clipping\\n    if images.dtype == torch.uint8:\\n        resized_images = torch.round(resized_images).clamp(0, 255).to(torch.uint8)\\n    elif images.dtype == torch.float32:\\n        resized_images = resized_images.clamp(0.0, 1.0)\\n    else:\\n        raise ValueError(f\\\"Unsupported image dtype: {images.dtype}\\\")\\n\\n    # Calculate padding\\n    pad_h0, remainder_h = divmod(height - resized_height, 2)\\n    pad_h1 = pad_h0 + remainder_h\\n    pad_w0, remainder_w = divmod(width - resized_width, 2)\\n    pad_w1 = pad_w0 + remainder_w\\n\\n    # Action queue logic for n_action_steps > 1\\n        if len(self._action_queue) == 0:\\n            actions = self.predict_action_chunk(batch)[:, : self.config.n_action_steps]\\n            # Transpose to get shape (n_action_steps, batch_size, action_dim)\\n            self._action_queue.extend(actions.transpose(0, 1))\\n\\n        return self._action_queue.popleft()\\n\\n    @torch.no_grad()\\n    def predict_action_chunk(self, batch: dict[str, Tensor], **kwargs: Unpack[ActionSelectKwargs]) -> Tensor:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Predict a chunk of actions given environment observations.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        self.eval()\\n\\n        # Prepare inputs\\n        images, img_masks = self._preprocess_images(batch)\\n        tokens, masks = batch[f\\\"{OBS_LANGUAGE_TOKENS}\\\"], batch[f\\\"{OBS_LANGUAGE_ATTENTION_MASK}\\\"]\\n\\n        # Sample actions using the model (pass through RTC kwargs, no separate state needed for PI05)\\n        actions = self.model.sample_actions(images, img_masks, tokens, masks, **kwargs)\\n\\n        # Unpad actions to actual action dimension\\n        original_action_dim = self.config.output_features[ACTION].shape[0]\\n        actions = actions[:, :, :original_action_dim]\\n\\n        return actions\\n\\n    def forward(self, batch: dict[str, Tensor], reduction: str = \\\"mean\\\") -> tuple[Tensor, dict]:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Run the batch through the model and compute the loss for training.\\n\\n        Args:\\n            batch: Training batch containing observations and actions.\\n            reduction: How to reduce the loss. Options:\\n                - \\\"mean\\\": Return scalar mean loss (default, backward compatible)\\n                - \\\"none\\\": Return per-sample losses of shape (batch_size,) for RA-BC weighting\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        # Prepare inputs\\n        images, img_masks = self._preprocess_images(batch)\\n        tokens, masks = batch[f\\\"{OBS_LANGUAGE_TOKENS}\\\"], batch[f\\\"{OBS_LANGUAGE_ATTENTION_MASK}\\\"]\\n\\n        actions = self.prepare_action(batch)\\n\\n        noise = self.model.sample_noise(actions.shape, actions.device)\\n        time = self.model.sample_time(actions.shape[0], actions.device)\\n\\n        # Compute loss (no separate state needed for PI05)\\n        losses = self.model.forward(images, img_masks, tokens, masks, actions, noise, time)\\n\\n        # Truncate losses to actual action dimensions\\n        original_action_dim = self.config.output_features[ACTION].shape[0]\\n        losses = losses[:, :, :original_action_dim]\\n\\n        loss_dict = {\\n            \\\"loss_per_dim\\\": losses.mean(dim=[0, 1]).detach().cpu().numpy().tolist(),\\n        }\\n\\n        if reduction == \\\"none\\\":\\n            # Return per-sample losses (B,) by averaging over time and action dims\\n            per_sample_loss = losses.mean(dim=(1, 2))\\n            loss_dict[\\\"loss\\\"] = per_sample_loss.mean().item()\\n            return per_sample_loss, loss_dict\\n        else:\\n            # Default: return scalar mean loss\\n            loss = losses.mean()\\n            loss_dict[\\\"loss\\\"] = loss.item()\\n            return loss, loss_dict\\n\\n    def _get_default_peft_targets(self) -> dict[str, any]:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Return default PEFT target modules for PI0.5 fine-tuning.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        common_projections = (\\n            \\\"state_proj|action_in_proj|action_out_proj|action_time_mlp_in|action_time_mlp_out\\\"\\n        )\\n        target_modules = rf\\\"(.*\\\\.gemma_expert\\\\..*\\\\.self_attn\\\\.(q|v)_proj|model\\\\.({common_projections}))\\\"\\n        return {\\n            \\\"target_modules\\\": target_modules,\\n            \\\"modules_to_save\\\": [],\\n        }\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.51}}"}

Check if input is in channels-last format [*b, h, w, c] or channels-first [*b, c, h, w]\\n    if images.shape[-1] <= 4:  # Assume channels-last format\\n        channels_last = True\\n        if images.dim() == 3:\\n            images = images.unsqueeze(0)  # Add batch dimension\\n        images = images.permute(0, 3, 1, 2)  # [b, h, w, c] -> [b, c, h, w]\\n    else:\\n        channels_last = False\\n        if images.dim() == 3:\\n            images = images.unsqueeze(0)  # Add batch dimension\\n\\n    batch_size, channels, cur_height, cur_width = images.shape\\n\\n    # Calculate resize ratio\\n    ratio = max(cur_width / width, cur_height / height)\\n    resized_height = int(cur_height / ratio)\\n    resized_width = int(cur_width / ratio)\\n\\n    # Resize\\n    resized_images = F.interpolate(\\n        images,\\n        size=(resized_height, resized_width),\\n        mode=mode,\\n        align_corners=False if mode == \\\"bilinear\\\" else None,\\n    )\\n\\n    # Handle dtype-specific clipping\\n    if images.dtype == torch.uint8:\\n        resized_images = torch.round(resized_images).clamp(0, 255).to(torch.uint8)\\n    elif images.dtype == torch.float32:\\n        resized_images = resized_images.clamp(0.0, 1.0)\\n    else:\\n        raise ValueError(f\\\"Unsupported image dtype: {images.dtype}\\\")\\n\\n    # Calculate padding\\n    pad_h0, remainder_h = divmod(height - resized_height, 2)\\n    pad_h1 = pad_h0 + remainder_h\\n    pad_w0, remainder_w = divmod(width - resized_width, 2)\\n    pad_w1 = pad_w0 + remainder_w\\n\\n    # Pad\\n    constant_value = 0 if images.dtype == torch.uint8 else 0.0\\n    padded_images = F.pad(\\n        resized_images,\\n        (pad_w0, pad_w1, pad_h0, pad_h1),  # left, right, top, bottom\\n        mode=\\\"constant\\\",\\n        value=constant_value,\\n    )\\n\\n    # Convert back to original format if needed\\n    if channels_last:\\n        padded_images = padded_images.permute(0, 2, 3, 1)  # [b, c, h, w] -> [b, h, w, c]\\n\\n    return padded_images\\n\\n\\n# Define the complete layer computation function for gradient checkpointing\\ndef compute_layer_complete(\\n    layer_idx, inputs_embeds, attention_mask, position_ids, adarms_cond, paligemma, gemma_expert\\n):\\n    models = [paligemma.model.language_model, gemma_expert.model]\\n    query_states = []\\n    key_states = []\\n    value_states = []\\n    gates = []\\n    for i, hidden_states in enumerate(inputs_embeds):\\n        layer = models[i].layers[layer_idx]\\n        hidden_states, gate = layernorm_forward(layer.input_layernorm, hidden_states, adarms_cond[i])\\n        gates.append(gate)\\n        input_shape = hidden_states.shape[:-1]\\n        hidden_shape = (*input_shape, -1, layer.self_attn.head_dim)\\n        query_state = layer.self_attn.q_proj(hidden_states).view(hidden_shape).transpose(1, 2)\\n        key_state = layer.self_attn.k_proj(hidden_states).view(hidden_shape).transpose(1, 2)\\n        value_state = layer.self_attn.v_proj(hidden_states).view(hidden_shape).transpose(1, 2)\\n        query_states.append(query_state)\\n        key_states.append(key_state)\\n        value_states.append(value_state)\\n    # Concatenate and process attention\\n    query_states = torch.cat(query_states, dim=2)\\n    key_states = torch.cat(key_states, dim=2)\\n    value_states = torch.cat(value_states, dim=2)\\n    dummy_tensor = torch.zeros(\\n        query_states.shape[0],\\n        query_states.shape[2],\\n        query_states.shape[-1],\\n        device=query_\\n\\n... [截断: 输出过长，省略 39620 字符] ...\\n\\n       images.append(img)\\n            img_masks.append(mask)\\n\\n        return images, img_masks\\n\\n    def prepare_action(self, batch):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Pad action\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        actions = pad_vector(batch[ACTION], self.config.max_action_dim)\\n        return actions\\n\\n    @torch.no_grad()\\n    def select_action(self, batch: dict[str, Tensor]) -> Tensor:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Select a single action given environment observations.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        assert not self._rtc_enabled(), (\\n            \\\"RTC is not supported for select_action, use it with predict_action_chunk\\\"\\n        )\\n\\n        self.eval()\\n\\n        # Action queue logic for n_action_steps > 1\\n        if len(self._action_queue) == 0:\\n            actions = self.predict_action_chunk(batch)[:, : self.config.n_action_steps]\\n            # Transpose to get shape (n_action_steps, batch_size, action_dim)\\n            self._action_queue.extend(actions.transpose(0, 1))\\n\\n        return self._action_queue.popleft()\\n\\n    @torch.no_grad()\\n    def predict_action_chunk(self, batch: dict[str, Tensor], **kwargs: Unpack[ActionSelectKwargs]) -> Tensor:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Predict a chunk of actions given environment observations.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        self.eval()\\n\\n        # Prepare inputs\\n        images, img_masks = self._preprocess_images(batch)\\n        tokens, masks = batch[f\\\"{OBS_LANGUAGE_TOKENS}\\\"], batch[f\\\"{OBS_LANGUAGE_ATTENTION_MASK}\\\"]\\n\\n        # Sample actions using the model (pass through RTC kwargs, no separate state needed for PI05)\\n        actions = self.model.sample_actions(images, img_masks, tokens, masks, **kwargs)\\n\\n        # Unpad actions to actual action dimension\\n        original_action_dim = self.config.output_features[ACTION].shape[0]\\n        actions = actions[:, :, :original_action_dim]\\n\\n        return actions\\n\\n    def forward(self, batch: dict[str, Tensor], reduction: str = \\\"mean\\\") -> tuple[Tensor, dict]:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Run the batch through the model and compute the loss for training.\\n\\n        Args:\\n            batch: Training batch containing observations and actions.\\n            reduction: How to reduce the loss. Options:\\n                - \\\"mean\\\": Return scalar mean loss (default, backward compatible)\\n                - \\\"none\\\": Return per-sample losses of shape (batch_size,) for RA-BC weighting\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        # Prepare inputs\\n        images, img_masks = self._preprocess_images(batch)\\n        tokens, masks = batch[f\\\"{OBS_LANGUAGE_TOKENS}\\\"], batch[f\\\"{OBS_LANGUAGE_ATTENTION_MASK}\\\"]\\n\\n        actions = self.prepare_action(batch)\\n\\n        noise = self.model.sample_noise(actions.shape, actions.device)\\n        time = self.model.sample_time(actions.shape[0], actions.device)\\n\\n        # Compute loss (no separate state needed for PI05)\\n        losses = self.model.forward(images, img_masks, tokens, masks, actions, noise, time)\\n\\n        # Truncate losses to actual action dimensions\\n        original_action_dim = self.config.output_features[ACTION].shape[0]\\n        losses = losses[:, :, :original_action_dim]\\n\\n        loss_dict = {\\n            \\\"loss_per_dim\\\": losses.mean(dim=[0, 1]).detach().cpu().numpy().tolist(),\\n        }\\n\\n        if reduction == \\\"none\\\":\\n            # Return per-sample losses (B,) by averaging over time and action dims\\n            per_sample_loss = losses.mean(dim=(1, 2))\\n            loss_dict[\\\"loss\\\"] = per_sample_loss.mean().item()\\n            return per_sample_loss, loss_dict\\n        else:\\n            # Default: return scalar mean loss\\n            loss = losses.mean()\\n            loss_dict[\\\"loss\\\"] = loss.item()\\n            return loss, loss_dict\\n\\n    def _get_default_peft_targets(self) -> dict[str, any]:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Return default PEFT target modules for PI0.5 fine-tuning.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        common_projections = (\\n            \\\"state_proj|action_in_proj|action_out_proj|action_time_mlp_in|action_time_mlp_out\\\"\\n        )\\n        target_modules = rf\\\"(.*\\\\.gemma_expert\\\\..*\\\\.self_attn\\\\.(q|v)_proj|model\\\\.({common_projections}))\\\"\\n        return {\\n            \\\"target_modules\\\": target_modules,\\n            \\\"modules_to_save\\\": [],\\n        }\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.51}}"

So it auto-detects channels-first vs channels-last format based on whether the last dimension is ≤ 4 (typical channel count).

When the wrapper passes channels-first directly to `resize_with_pad_torch`, the function detects this based on the last dimension being larger than 4, treating it as channels-first and resizing accordingly without any permutation. The official pipeline, by contrast, explicitly converts to channels-last before resizing, then back to channels-first after—which should produce the same result, but the wrapper's implicit detection might handle edge cases differently. float32 [0, 1]
